\chi_{(\Delta(P,\phi,Q),e\otimes f^{\ast})}((us,vt)\epsilon)=0

if
\Delta(P,\phi,Q)\langle(u,v)\rangle\notin S_{p}^{\Delta}(G\times H)
.

Corollary 7.8
.

Let
G
and
H
be finite groups and let
A\in\operatorname{Bl}(\mathcal{O}G)
,
B\in\operatorname{Bl}(\mathcal{O}H)
. The composite
\pi\alpha
is injective on
T^{\Delta}(A,B)
. The image of
T^{\Delta}(A,B)
under
\pi\alpha
is equal to the subgroup of tuples
(\chi_{(\Delta(P,\phi,Q),e\otimes f^{\ast})})
in

(\prod_{(\Delta(P,\phi,Q),e\otimes f^{\ast})\in\mathcal{BP}_{\mathcal{O}}^{\Delta}(A\otimes B^{\ast})}R_{\mathbb{K}}(Y_{(\Delta(P,\phi,Q),e\otimes f^{\ast})}/\Delta(P,\phi,Q),e\otimes f^{\ast}))^{G\times H}

that satisfy: for all
(\Delta(P,\phi,Q),e\otimes f^{\ast})\in\mathcal{BP}_{\mathcal{O}}^{\Delta}(A\otimes B^{\ast})
, all
p
-elements
(u,v)\in Y_{(\Delta(P,\phi,Q),e\otimes f^{\ast})}
, and all
(s,t)\in C_{Y_{(\Delta(P,\phi,Q),e\otimes f^{\ast})}}(u,v)_{p^{\prime}}
one has

\chi_{(\Delta(P,\phi,Q),e\otimes f^{\ast})}(us,vt)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}e^{\prime}\in\operatorname{bli}(\mathcal{O}C_{G}(P\langle u\rangle))\\
(P,e)\trianglelefteq(P\langle u\rangle,e^{\prime})\\
s\in N_{G}(P\langle u\rangle,e^{\prime})\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}f^{\prime}\in\operatorname{bli}(\mathcal{O}C_{H}(Q\langle v\rangle))\\
(Q,f)\trianglelefteq(Q\langle v\rangle,f^{\prime})\\
t\in N_{H}(Q\langle v\rangle,f^{\prime})\end{subarray}}\chi_{(\Delta(P,\phi,Q)\langle(u,v)\rangle,e^{\prime}\otimes f^{\prime\ast})}(s,t)

if
\Delta(P,\phi,Q)\langle(u,v)\rangle\in S_{p}^{\Delta}(G\times H)
, and

\chi_{(\Delta(P,\phi,Q),e\otimes f^{\ast})}(us,vt)=0

if
\Delta(P,\phi,Q)\langle(u,v)\rangle\notin S_{p}^{\Delta}(G\times H)
.

Let
G
and
H
be finite groups, let
A\in\operatorname{Bl}(\mathcal{O}G)
and let
B\in\operatorname{Bl}(\mathcal{O}H)
. Let
(D,e_{D})\in\mathcal{BP}_{\mathcal{O}}(A)
be a maximal
A
-Brauer pair and let
(E,f_{E})\in\mathcal{BP}_{\mathcal{O}}(B)
be a maximal
B
-Brauer pair. If
P\leq D
write
e_{P}
for the unique block idempotent of
\mathcal{O}C_{G}(P)
such that
(P,e_{P})\leq(D,e_{D})
. Likewise if
Q\leq E
write
(Q,f_{Q})\leq(E,f_{E})
. Recall that
(E,f_{E}^{\ast})
is a maximal
B^{\ast}
-Brauer pair and if
Q\leq E
then
(Q,f_{Q}^{\ast})\leq(E,f_{E}^{\ast})
. By Lemma
2.11

(D\times E,e_{D}\otimes f_{E}^{\ast})
is a maximal
A\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}B^{\ast}
-Brauer pair. If
R\leq D\times E
then

(R,e_{p_{1}(R)}\otimes f_{p_{2}(R)}^{\ast})\leq(D\times E,e_{D}\otimes f_{E}^{\ast})

is a containment of
A\otimes B^{\ast}
-Brauer pairs by Lemma
2.12
. Let
\mathcal{A}=\mathcal{F}_{(D,e_{D})}(A,G)
and let
\mathcal{B}^{\ast}=\mathcal{F}_{(E,f_{E}^{\ast})}(B^{\ast},H)
. Note that
\mathcal{B}^{\ast}=\mathcal{F}_{(E,f_{E})}(B,H)
— we prefer to write
\mathcal{B}^{\ast}
for consistency of notation. By Lemma
2.12
we have

\mathcal{F}_{(D\times E),e_{D}\otimes f_{E}^{\ast})}(A\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}B^{\ast},G\times H)=\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{B}^{\ast}.

For each
R\leq D\times E
set

Y_{R}:=Y_{(R,e_{p_{1}(R)}\otimes f_{p_{2}(R)}^{\ast})}=N_{G\times H}(R,e_{p_{1}(R)}\otimes f_{p_{2}(R)}^{\ast}).

Recall from Section
6
that we have an injective group homomorphism

\delta_{A\otimes B^{\ast}}=\delta:T(A,B)\hookrightarrow\left(\prod_{R\leq D\times E}R_{\mathbb{K}}(Y_{R}/R,e_{p_{1}(R)}\otimes f_{p_{2}(R)}^{\ast})\right)^{\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{B}^{\ast}}.

If
M\in{}_{A\otimes B^{\ast}}{\textbf{triv}}
then

\delta([M])=(\chi_{M(R,e_{p_{1}(R)}\otimes f_{p_{2}(R)}^{\ast})})_{R\leq D\times E}.

Theorem 7.9
.

Let
G
and
H
be finite groups, let
A\in\operatorname{Bl}(\mathcal{O}G)
and
B\in\operatorname{Bl}(\mathcal{O}H)
and choose maximal Brauer pairs
(D,e_{D})\in\mathcal{BP}_{\mathcal{O}}(A)
,
(E,f_{E})\in\mathcal{BP}_{\mathcal{O}}(B)
. With the notation set above,

\delta(T^{\Delta}(A,B))=\left\{(\chi_{R})_{R\leq D\times E}\in\delta(T(A,B))|\chi_{R}=0\text{ if }R\notin S_{p}^{\Delta}(G\times H)\right\}.

Proof.

Recall that we have a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\left(\prod_{(R,e\otimes f^{\ast})\in\mathcal{BP}_{\mathcal{O}}(A\otimes B^{\ast})}R_{\mathbb{K}}(Y_{(R,e\otimes f^{\ast})}/R,e\otimes f^{\ast})\right)^{G\times H}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\left(\prod_{R\leq D\times E}R_{\mathbb{K}}(Y_{R}/R,e_{p_{1}(R)}\otimes f_{p_{2}(R)}^{\ast})\right)^{\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{B}^{\ast}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T(A,B)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta}

In particular,
\delta(T^{\Delta}(A,B))=\pi(\alpha(T^{\Delta}(A,B)))
. By Theorem
7.4
we have that

\alpha(T^{\Delta}(A,B))=\left\{(\chi_{(R,e\otimes f^{\ast})})\in\alpha(T(A,B))|\chi_{(R,e\otimes f^{\ast})}=0\text{ if }R\notin S_{p}^{\Delta}(G\times H)\right\}.

Now let
(\chi_{(R,e\otimes f^{\ast})})\in\alpha(T^{\Delta}(A,B))
. Then

\pi((\chi_{(R,e\otimes f^{\ast})}))=(\chi_{(R,e\otimes f^{\ast})})_{R\leq D\times E}\in\delta(T(A,B))

and if
R\leq D\times E
is such that
R\notin S_{p}^{\Delta}(G\times H)
then
\chi_{(R,e\otimes f^{\ast})}=0
. It follows that

\delta(T^{\Delta}(A,B))\subseteq\left\{(\chi_{R})_{R\leq D\times E}\in\delta(T(A,B))|\chi_{R}=0\text{ if }R\notin S_{p}^{\Delta}(G\times H)\right\}.

Conversely, let
(\chi_{R})_{R\leq D\times E}\in\delta(T(A,B))
be such that
\chi_{R}=0
if
R\notin S_{p}^{\Delta}(G\times H)
. Set
(\psi_{(R,e\otimes f^{\ast})})=\pi^{-1}((\chi_{R}))
. Recall from the proof of Proposition
6.7
that if
(R,e\otimes f^{\ast})\in\mathcal{BP}_{\mathcal{O}}(A\otimes B^{\ast})
then

\psi_{(R,e\otimes f^{\ast})}={}^{(g,h)^{-1}}\chi_{{}^{(g,h)}R}

where
(g,h)\in G\times H
is an element satisfying
{}^{(g,h)}(R,e\otimes f^{\ast})\leq(D\times E,e_{D}\otimes f_{E}^{\ast})
. Since
(\chi_{R})\in\delta(T(A,B))=\pi(\alpha(T(A,B)))
we have that
(\psi_{(R,e\otimes f^{\ast})})\in\alpha(T(A,B))
. If
(R,e\otimes f^{\ast})
is an
A\otimes B^{\ast}
-Brauer pair such that
R\notin S_{p}^{\Delta}(G\times H)
then
\psi_{(R,e\otimes f^{\ast})}={}^{(g,h)^{-1}}\chi_{{}^{(g,h)}R}=0
since
\chi_{{}^{(g,h)}R}=0
. Thus we have that
(\psi_{(R,e\otimes f^{\ast})})\in\alpha(T^{\Delta}(A,B))
, and so
(\chi_{R})\in\pi(\alpha(T^{\Delta}(A,B)))=\delta(T^{\Delta}(A,B))
. The proof is complete.
∎

The corollary below follows immediately from Theorems
6.8
and
7.9
.

Corollary 7.10
.

Let
G
and
H
be finite groups,
A\in\operatorname{Bl}(\mathcal{O}G)
,
B\in\operatorname{Bl}(\mathcal{O}H)
. Let
(D,e_{D})
and
(E,f_{E})
be maximal Brauer pairs (over
\mathcal{O}
) for
A
and
B
, respectively. For each
P\leq D
write
e_{P}
for the unique block idempotent of
\mathcal{O}C_{G}(P)
such that
(P,e_{P})\leq(D,e_{D})
. Similarly, for each
Q\leq E
write
(Q,f_{Q})\leq(E,f_{E})
. Set
\mathcal{A}=\mathcal{F}_{(D,e_{D})}(A,G)
and
\mathcal{B}^{\ast}=\mathcal{F}_{(E,f_{E}^{\ast})}(B^{\ast},H)
. For each
R\leq D\times E
set
Y_{R}=Y_{(R,e_{p_{1}(R)}\otimes f_{p_{2}(R)}^{\ast})}=N_{G\times H}(R,e_{p_{1}(R)}\otimes f_{p_{2}(R)}^{\ast})
. Then the image of
T^{\Delta}(A,B)
under the map

\delta:T(A,B)\hookrightarrow\left(\prod_{R\leq D\times E}R_{\mathbb{K}}(Y_{R}/R,e_{p_{1}(R)}\otimes f_{p_{2}(R)}^{\ast})\right)^{\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{B}^{\ast}}.

is equal to the subgroup of tuples
(\chi_{R})\in\left(\prod_{R\leq D\times E}R_{\mathbb{K}}(Y_{R}/R,e_{p_{1}(R)}\otimes f_{p_{2}(R)}^{\ast})\right)^{\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{B}^{\ast}}
that satisfy
\chi_{R}=0
if
R\notin S_{p}^{\Delta}(G\times H)
and

\chi_{R}((us,vt)\epsilon_{\langle(u,v)\rangle})=\operatorname{Ind}_{C_{Y_{R}\cap Y_{R\langle(u,v)\rangle}}(u,v)}^{C_{Y_{R}}(u,v)}(\operatorname{Res}_{C_{Y_{R}\cap Y_{R\langle(u,v)\rangle}}(u,v)}^{Y_{R\langle(u,v)\rangle}}(\chi_{R\langle(u,v)\rangle}))(s,t)

for all
R\leq D\times E
,
(u,v)\in N_{D\times E}(R)
, and
(s,t)\in C_{Y_{R}}(u,v)_{p^{\prime}}
, where

\epsilon_{\langle(u,v)\rangle}=\operatorname{tr}_{C_{Y_{R}\cap Y_{R\langle(u,v)\rangle}}(u,v)}^{C_{Y_{R}}(u,v)}(e_{p_{1}(R)\langle u\rangle}\otimes f_{p_{2}(R)\langle v\rangle}^{\ast}).

8
Strong isotypies and
p
-permutation equivalences

Throughout this section
G
and
H
are finite groups,
(\mathbb{K},\mathcal{O},F)
is a
p
-modular system large enough for
G\times H
,
A
is a block of
\mathcal{O}G
, and
B
is a block of
\mathcal{O}H
. The purpose of this section is to introduce a new type of block equivalence that we call a
strong isotypy
and to compare this definition with that of a
p
-permutation equivalence.

Hypotheses 8.1
.

Let
A\in\operatorname{Bl}(\mathcal{O}G)
and
B\in\operatorname{Bl}(\mathcal{O}H)
. Let
(D,e_{D})\in\mathcal{BP}_{\mathcal{O}}(A)
and
(E,f_{E})\in\mathcal{BP}_{\mathcal{O}}(B)
be maximal Brauer pairs for
A
and
B
, respectively. For each subgroup
P\leq D
(respectively,
Q\leq E
) write
e_{P}
(resp.
f_{Q}
) for the unique block idempotent of
\mathcal{O}C_{G}(P)
(resp.
\mathcal{O}C_{H}(Q)
) such that
(P,e_{P})\leq(D,e_{D})
(resp.
(Q,f_{Q})\leq(E,f_{E})
). Set
\mathcal{A}=\mathcal{F}_{(D,e_{D})}(G,A)
, set
\mathcal{B}=\mathcal{F}_{(E,f_{E})}(H,B)
, and let
\phi:E\overset{\sim}{\to}D
be a group isomorphism that induces an isomorphism of fusion systems
\phi:\mathcal{B}\overset{\sim}{\to}\mathcal{A}
. For each
Q\leq E
set

\displaystyle Y_{Q}

\displaystyle=N_{G\times H}(\Delta(\phi(Q),\phi,Q),e_{\phi(Q)}\otimes f_{Q}^{\ast}),

\displaystyle J_{Q}

\displaystyle=N_{H}(Q,f_{Q})

and for each
P\leq D
set

I_{P}=N_{G}(P,e_{P}).

The definition below makes use of the notion of the “extended tensor product” of two characters. See
[
2
, Section 6]
for more information.

Definition 8.2
.

Assume Hypotheses
8.1
. Then a
strong isotypy between

A

and

B
(relative to
(D,e_{D})
,
(E,f_{E})
, and
\phi:E\overset{\sim}{\to}D
) is a family of virtual characters

\chi_{Q}\in R_{\mathbb{K}}(Y_{Q}/\Delta(\phi(Q),\phi,Q),e_{\phi(Q)}\otimes f_{Q}^{\ast}),\qquad Q\leq E

that satisfies each of the following conditions:

(1)

Let
Q_{1},Q_{2}\leq E
and set
P_{i}:=\phi(Q_{i})
,
i=1,2
. If
g\in G
,
h\in H
are such that
{}^{g}(P_{1},e_{P_{1}})=(P_{2},e_{P_{2}})
,
{}^{h}(Q_{1},f_{Q_{1}})=(Q_{2},f_{Q_{2}})
, and
c_{g}\phi=\phi c_{h}:Q_{1}\overset{\sim}{\to}P_{2}
, then
{}^{(g,h)}\chi_{Q_{1}}=\chi_{Q_{2}}
.

(2)

Let
Q\leq E
and set
P:=\phi(Q)
. Let
((u,v),\epsilon)\in\mathcal{BE}_{\mathcal{O}}(Y_{Q},e_{P}\otimes f_{Q}^{\ast})
be such that

(\langle(u,v)\rangle,\epsilon)\leq(N_{D\times E}(\Delta(P,\phi,Q)),e_{N_{D}(P)}\otimes f_{N_{E}(Q)}^{\ast})

and let
(s,t)\in C_{Y_{Q}}(u,v)_{p^{\prime}}
.

(a)

If
u=\phi(v)
then

\chi_{Q}((us,vt)\epsilon)=\operatorname{Ind}_{C_{Y_{Q}\cap Y_{Q\langle v\rangle}}(u,v)}^{C_{Y_{Q}}(u,v)}(\operatorname{Res}_{C_{Y_{Q}\cap Y_{Q\langle v\rangle}}(u,v)}^{Y_{Q\langle v\rangle}}(\chi_{Q\langle v\rangle}))(s,t).

(b)

If
\chi_{Q}((us,vt)\epsilon)\neq 0
then

(\Delta(P,\phi,Q)\langle(u,v)\rangle,e_{P\langle u\rangle}\otimes f_{Q\langle v\rangle}^{\ast})\leq_{G\times H}(\Delta(D,\phi,E),e_{D}\otimes f_{E}^{\ast})

or equivalently, there exists an
\mathcal{A}
-isomorphism
\alpha:P\langle u\rangle\overset{\sim}{\to}\alpha(P\langle u\rangle)
and a
\mathcal{B}
-isomorphism
\beta:Q\langle v\rangle\overset{\sim}{\to}\beta(Q\langle v\rangle)
such that
\alpha=\phi\beta\phi^{-1}:P\overset{\sim}{\to}\alpha(P)
and
\alpha(u)=\phi(\beta(v))
.

(3)

Let
Q\leq E
and set
P:=\phi(Q)
. Then

\chi_{Q}\overset{Y_{Q},Y_{Q}^{\circ}}{\underset{H}{\otimes}}\chi_{Q}^{\circ}=[\mathbb{K}C_{G}(P)e_{P}]\in R_{\mathbb{K}}(N_{G\times G}(\Delta(P),e_{P}\otimes e_{P}^{\ast})/\Delta(P),e_{P}\otimes e_{P}^{\ast})

and

\chi_{Q}^{\circ}\overset{Y_{Q}^{\circ},Y_{Q}}{\underset{G}{\otimes}}\chi_{Q}=[\mathbb{K}C_{H}(Q)f_{Q}]\in R_{\mathbb{K}}(N_{H\times H}(\Delta(Q),f_{Q}\otimes f_{Q}^{\ast})/\Delta(Q),f_{Q}\otimes f_{Q}^{\ast}).

Before continuing we analyze further the conclusion of condition (2b) in Definition
8.2
.

Lemma 8.3
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{B}
be saturated fusion systems over
D
and
E
, respectively, and let
\phi:E\overset{\sim}{\to}D
be a group isomorphism that induces an isomorphism of fusion systems
\phi:\mathcal{B}\overset{\sim}{\to}\mathcal{A}
. Let
Q\leq E
and set
P:=\phi(Q)
. Let
u\in N_{D}(P)
,
v\in N_{E}(Q)
and suppose that there exists an
\mathcal{A}
-isomorphism
\alpha:P\langle u\rangle\overset{\sim}{\to}\alpha(P\langle u\rangle)
and a
\mathcal{B}
-isomorphism
\beta:Q\langle v\rangle\overset{\sim}{\to}\beta(Q\langle v\rangle)
such that
\alpha=\phi\beta\phi^{-1}:P\overset{\sim}{\to}\alpha(P)
and
\alpha(u)=\phi(\beta(v))
. Then

(1)

There exists an
\mathcal{A}
-isomorphism
\psi:P\langle u\rangle\overset{\sim}{\to}P\langle\phi(v)\rangle
such that
\psi|_{P}=\operatorname{id}_{P}
and
\psi(u)=\phi(v)
. In particular,
u
and
\phi(v)
are
\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{A}}(P)
-conjugate.

(2)

There exists a
\mathcal{B}
-isomorphism
\omega:Q\langle v\rangle\overset{\sim}{\to}Q\langle\phi^{-1}(u)\rangle
such that
\omega|_{Q}=\operatorname{id}_{Q}
and
\omega(v)=\phi^{-1}(u)
. In particular,
v
and
\phi^{-1}(u)
are
\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{B}}(Q)
-conjugate.

Proof.

The assumptions imply that
\phi\beta^{-1}\phi^{-1}:\alpha(P\langle u\rangle)\overset{\sim}{\to}P\langle\phi(v)\rangle
is an
\mathcal{A}
-isomorphism. Then the
\mathcal{A}
-isomorphism
\psi=\phi\beta^{-1}\phi^{-1}\alpha:P\langle u\rangle\overset{\sim}{\to}P\langle\phi(v)\rangle
satisfies
\psi|_{P}=\operatorname{id}_{P}
and
\psi(u)=\phi(v)
. In particular,
\psi|_{\langle u\rangle}:\langle u\rangle\overset{\sim}{\to}\langle\phi(v)\rangle
is an
\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{A}}(P)
-isomorphism that maps
u
to
\phi(v)
. This shows that (1) holds. Setting
\omega=\phi^{-1}\psi^{-1}\phi:Q\langle v\rangle\overset{\sim}{\to}Q\langle\phi^{-1}(u)\rangle
gives (2).
∎

Corollary 8.4
.

Assume Hypotheses
8.1
and let
\chi_{Q}
,
Q\leq E
, be a strong isotypy between
A
and
B
. Let
Q\leq E
,
P:=\phi(Q)
, and let
((u,v),\epsilon)\in\mathcal{BE}_{\mathcal{O}}(Y_{Q},e_{P}\otimes f_{Q}^{\ast})
be such that

(\langle(u,v)\rangle,\epsilon)\leq(N_{D\times E}(\Delta(P,\phi,Q)),e_{N_{D}(P)}\otimes f_{N_{E}(Q)}^{\ast}).

If
u
and
\phi(v)
are not
\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{A}}(P)
-conjugate then
d_{Y_{Q}}^{(u,v),\epsilon}(\chi_{Q})=0
.

Proof.

If
u
and
\phi(v)
are not
\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{A}}(P)
-conjugate then Lemma
8.3
and (2b) of Definition
8.2
imply that
\chi_{Q}((us,vt)\epsilon)=0
for all
(s,t)\in C_{Y_{Q}}(u,v)_{p^{\prime}}
, hence
d_{Y_{Q}}^{(u,v),\epsilon}(\chi_{Q})=0
.
∎

Lemma 8.5
.

Let
A\in\operatorname{Bl}(\mathcal{O}G)
,
B\in\operatorname{Bl}(\mathcal{O}H)
, and let
\gamma\in T^{\Delta}(A,B)
be a
p
-permutation equivalence with maximal
\gamma
-Brauer pair
(\Delta(D,\phi,E),e_{D}\otimes f_{E}^{\ast})
. Then
(D,e_{D})\in\mathcal{BP}_{\mathcal{O}}(G,A)
and
(E,f_{E})\in\mathcal{BP}_{\mathcal{O}}(H,B)
are maximal Brauer pairs. For each
P\leq D
(respectively,
Q\leq E
) write
e_{P}
(resp.
f_{Q}
) for the unique block idempotent of
\mathcal{O}C_{G}(P)
(resp.
\mathcal{O}C_{H}(Q)
) such that
(P,e_{P})\leq(D,e_{D})
(resp.
(Q,f_{Q})\leq(E,f_{E})
). Set
\mathcal{A}=\mathcal{F}_{(D,e_{D})}(G,A)
and set
\mathcal{B}=\mathcal{F}_{(E,f_{E})}(H,B)
. Then
\phi:E\overset{\sim}{\to}D
induces a fusion system isomorphism
\phi:\mathcal{B}\overset{\sim}{\to}\mathcal{A}
. For each
Q\leq E
set

Y_{Q}:=N_{G\times H}(\Delta(\phi(Q),\phi,Q),e_{\phi(Q)}\otimes f_{Q}^{\ast})

and set

\chi_{Q}:=\chi_{\gamma(\Delta(\phi(Q),\phi,Q),e_{\phi(Q)}\otimes f_{Q}^{\ast})}\in R_{\mathbb{K}}(Y_{Q}/\Delta(\phi(Q),\phi,Q),e_{\phi(Q)}\otimes f_{Q}^{\ast}).

Then the characters
\chi_{Q}
,
Q\leq E
, form a strong isotypy between
A
and
B
.

Proof.

That
(D,e_{D})
and
(E,f_{E})
are maximal Brauer pairs for
A
and
B
, respectively, follows from
[
2
, Theorem 10.11(c)]
, and
\phi:E\overset{\sim}{\to}D
induces an isomorphism of fusion systems
\phi:\mathcal{B}\overset{\sim}{\to}\mathcal{A}
by
[
2
, Theorem 11.2]
. Recall that if
\mathcal{B}^{\ast}:=\mathcal{F}_{(E,f_{E}^{\ast})}(H,B^{\ast})
then
\mathcal{B}=\mathcal{B}^{\ast}
and also
\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{B}^{\ast}=\mathcal{F}_{(D\times E,e_{D}\otimes f_{E}^{\ast})}(G\times H,A\otimes B^{\ast})
by Lemma
2.12
. Write
(\theta_{R})_{R\leq D\times E}
for the image of
\gamma
under the map
\delta
of Theorem
7.9
and Corollary
7.10
. Note that if
Q\leq E
then
\chi_{Q}=\theta_{\Delta(\phi(Q),\phi,Q)}
.

We first verify condition (1) of Definition
8.2
. Let
Q_{1},Q_{2}\leq E
and set
P_{i}=\phi(Q_{i})
for
i=1,2
. Let
g\in G
,
h\in H
be such that
{}^{g}(P_{1},e_{P_{1}})=(P_{2},e_{P_{2}})
,
{}^{h}(Q_{1},f_{Q_{1}})=(Q_{2},f_{Q_{2}})
, and
c_{g}\phi=\phi c_{h}:Q_{1}\overset{\sim}{\to}P_{2}
. Note that for
i=1,2

(\Delta(P_{i},\phi,Q_{i}),e_{P_{i}}\otimes f_{Q_{i}}^{\ast})\leq(D\times E,e_{D}\otimes f_{E}^{\ast})

is a containment of
A\otimes B^{\ast}
-Brauer pairs and

{}^{(g,h)}(\Delta(P_{1},\phi,Q_{1}),e_{P_{1}}\otimes f_{Q_{1}}^{\ast})=(\Delta(P_{2},\phi,Q_{2}),e_{P_{2}}\otimes f_{Q_{2}}^{\ast}),

so
c_{(g,h)}:\Delta(P_{1},\phi,Q_{1})\overset{\sim}{\to}\Delta(P_{2},\phi,Q_{2})
is an
\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{B}^{\ast}
-isomorphism. Since the character tuple
(\theta_{R})_{R\leq D\times E}
is
\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{B}^{\ast}
-fixed it follows that
{}^{(g,h)}\theta_{\Delta(P_{1},\phi,Q_{1})}=\theta_{\Delta(P_{2},\phi,Q_{2})}
. In other words
{}^{(g,h)}\chi_{Q_{1}}=\chi_{Q_{2}}
, as needed.

Next we verify (2). Let
Q\leq E
and set
P=\phi(Q)
. Let
((u,v),\epsilon)\in\mathcal{BE}_{\mathcal{O}}(Y_{Q},e_{P}\otimes f_{Q}^{\ast})
be such that

(\langle(u,v)\rangle,\epsilon)\leq(N_{D\times E}(\Delta(P,\phi,Q)),e_{N_{D}(P)}\otimes f_{N_{E}(Q)}^{\ast})

and let
(s,t)\in C_{Y_{Q}}(u,v)_{p^{\prime}}
. First we prove (a). Assume that
u=\phi(v)
. Lemma
2.10
implies that

(\langle(u,v)\rangle,\operatorname{tr}_{C_{Y_{Q}\cap Y_{Q\langle v\rangle}}(u,v)}^{C_{Y_{Q}}(u,v)}(e_{P\langle u\rangle}\otimes f_{Q\langle v\rangle}^{\ast}))

is an
\mathcal{O}Y_{Q}(e_{P}\otimes f_{Q}^{\ast})
-Brauer pair contained in
(N_{D\times E}(\Delta(P,\phi,Q)),e_{N_{D}(P)}\otimes f_{N_{E}(Q)}^{\ast})
, hence

\epsilon=\operatorname{tr}_{C_{Y_{Q}\cap Y_{Q\langle v\rangle}}(u,v)}^{C_{Y_{Q}}(u,v)}(e_{P\langle u\rangle}\otimes f_{Q\langle v\rangle}^{\ast})

by the uniqueness of Brauer pairs contained in a fixed Brauer pair. The equality

\chi_{Q}((us,vt)\epsilon)=\operatorname{Ind}_{C_{Y_{Q}\cap Y_{Q\langle v\rangle}}(u,v)}^{C_{Y_{Q}}(u,v)}(\operatorname{Res}_{C_{Y_{Q}\cap Y_{Q\langle v\rangle}}(u,v)}^{Y_{Q\langle v\rangle}}(\chi_{Q\langle v\rangle}))(s,t).

then follows from the coherence condition of Corollary
7.10
applied at
\Delta(P,\phi,Q)
,
(u,v)
, and
(s,t)
. This proves (a) and we move on to (b). Assume that
\chi_{Q}((us,vt)\epsilon)\neq 0
. Then the coherence condition of Corollary
7.10
implies that
\theta_{\Delta(P,\phi,Q)\langle(u,v)\rangle}\neq 0
, hence
\gamma(\Delta(P,\phi,Q)\langle(u,v)\rangle,e_{P\langle u\rangle}\otimes f_{Q\langle v\rangle}^{\ast})\neq 0
. Thus
(\Delta(P,\phi,Q)\langle(u,v)\rangle,e_{P\langle u\rangle}\otimes f_{Q\langle v\rangle}^{\ast})
is a
\gamma
-Brauer pair. Since any two maximal
\gamma
-Brauer pairs are
G\times H
-conjugate (see
[
2
, Theorem 10.11(b)]
) we have

(\Delta(P,\phi,Q)\langle(u,v)\rangle,e_{P\langle u\rangle}\otimes f_{Q\langle v\rangle}^{\ast})\leq_{G\times H}(\Delta(D,\phi,E),e_{D}\otimes f_{E}^{\ast}).

This proves (b) and completes the verification of (2).

Finally, the equalities in condition (3) of Definition
8.2
follow from
[
2
, Propositions 11.8, 11.9]
, noting that

Y_{Q}\ast Y_{Q}^{\circ}=N_{G\times G}(\Delta(P),e_{P}\otimes e_{P}^{\ast})

and

Y_{Q}^{\circ}\ast Y_{Q}=N_{H\times H}(\Delta(Q),f_{Q}\otimes f_{Q}^{\ast}).

∎

Lemma 8.6
.

Let
B\in\operatorname{Bl}(\mathcal{O}G)
and let
0\neq\gamma\in T_{\mathcal{O}}(B)
. Set

\displaystyle X(\gamma)

\displaystyle:=\left\{(P,e)\in\mathcal{BP}_{\mathcal{O}}(B)|\chi_{\gamma(P,e)}\neq 0\right\}

\displaystyle Y(\gamma)

\displaystyle:=\left\{(P,e)\in\mathcal{BP}_{\mathcal{O}}(B)|\gamma(P,e)\neq 0\right\}

\displaystyle Z(\gamma)

\displaystyle:=\left\{(P,e)\in\mathcal{BP}_{\mathcal{O}}(B)|M(P,e)\neq 0\text{ for some indecomposable }M\in\operatorname{supp}(\gamma)\right\}.

(Here
\operatorname{supp}(\gamma)
denotes the support of
\gamma
, which is the set of isomorphism classes of indecomposable trivial source
B
-modules appearing in
\gamma
with nonzero coefficient when
\gamma
is written as a linear combination of the standard basis elements of
T_{\mathcal{O}}(B)
.) Then

\max X(\gamma)=\max Y(\gamma)=\max Z(\gamma).

Proof.

Recall that if
(Q,f)\in\mathcal{BP}_{\mathcal{O}}(B)
then the Brauer construction
-(Q,f):T_{\mathcal{O}}(B)\to T_{\mathcal{O}}(N_{G}(Q,f),f)
restricts to an injective map from the span of the isomorphism classes
[M]
of indecomposable trivial source
B
-modules
M
such that
(Q,f)
is a maximal
M
-Brauer pair to the span of the isomorphism classes of projective indecomposable
\mathcal{O}N_{G}(Q,f)f
-modules in
T_{\mathcal{O}}(N_{G}(Q,f),f)
.

It is clear that
X(\gamma)\subseteq Y(\gamma)\subseteq Z(\gamma)
. Let
(Q,f)\in\max Z(\gamma)
. Let
M
be any indecomposable module in the support of
\gamma
satisfying
M(Q,f)\neq 0
. Then
(Q,f)
is a maximal
M
-Brauer pair, for if
(Q,f)\leq(Q^{\prime},f^{\prime})
with
(Q^{\prime},f^{\prime})
a maximal
M
-Brauer pair then
(Q^{\prime},f^{\prime})\in Z(\gamma)
and hence
(Q,f)=(Q^{\prime},f^{\prime})
. Now write
\gamma=\sum a_{[M]}[M]
where
[M]
runs over the standard basis elements of
T_{\mathcal{O}}(B)
. Then

\displaystyle\gamma(Q,f)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}[M]\in\operatorname{supp}(\gamma)\\
M(Q,f)\neq 0\end{subarray}}a_{[M]}[M(Q,f)]=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}[M]\in\operatorname{supp}(\gamma)\\
(Q,f)\in\max\mathcal{BP}_{\mathcal{O}}(M)\end{subarray}}a_{[M]}[M(Q,f)]

\displaystyle=\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}[M]\in\operatorname{supp}(\gamma)\\
(Q,f)\in\max\mathcal{BP}_{\mathcal{O}}(M)\end{subarray}}a_{[M]}[M]\right)(Q,f).

By the remark in the first paragraph of this proof it follows that
\gamma(Q,f)\neq 0
and that
\chi_{\gamma(Q,f)}\neq 0
. Thus
(Q,f)\in Y(\gamma)
and
(Q,f)\in X(\gamma)
. Since
X(\gamma)
and
Y(\gamma)
are both subsets of
Z(\gamma)
it follows that
(Q,f)\in\max Y(\gamma)
and
(Q,f)\in\max X(\gamma)
. Since
(Q,f)
was an arbitrary maximal element of
Z(\gamma)
this gives

\max Z(\gamma)\subseteq\max Y(\gamma)\qquad\text{and}\qquad\max Z(\gamma)\subseteq\max X(\gamma).

The reverse containments follow easily from the fact that
X(\gamma)\subseteq Y(\gamma)\subseteq Z(\gamma)
.
∎

Lemma 8.7
.

Assume Hypotheses
8.1
and let
\chi_{Q}
,
Q\leq E
, be a strong isotypy between
A
and
B
. Then there exists a
p
-permutation equivalence
\gamma\in T^{\Delta}(A,B)
such that
(\Delta(D,\phi,E),e_{D}\otimes f_{E}^{\ast})
is a maximal
\gamma
-Brauer pair and

\chi_{Q}=\chi_{\gamma(\Delta(\phi(Q),\phi,Q),e_{\phi(Q)}\otimes f_{Q}^{\ast})}

for each
Q\leq E
.

Proof.

Recall that if
\mathcal{B}^{\ast}:=\mathcal{F}_{(E,f_{E}^{\ast})}(H,B^{\ast})
then
\mathcal{B}=\mathcal{B}^{\ast}
and also
\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{B}^{\ast}=\mathcal{F}_{(D\times E,e_{D}\otimes f_{E}^{\ast})}(G\times H,A\otimes B^{\ast})
by Lemma
2.12
. Recall also that if
R\leq D\times E
then

(R,e_{p_{1}(R)}\otimes f_{p_{2}(R)}^{\ast})\leq(D\times E,e_{D}\otimes f_{E}^{\ast})

is a containment of
A\otimes B^{\ast}
-Brauer pairs, again by Lemma
2.12
. For each
R\leq D\times E
set
X_{R}=N_{G\times H}(R,e_{p_{1}(R)}\otimes f_{p_{2}(R)}^{\ast})
. Note that if
Q\leq E
then
X_{\Delta(\phi(Q),\phi,Q)}=Y_{Q}
.

For each
R\leq D\times E
we define a (virtual) character

\theta_{R}\in R_{\mathbb{K}}(X_{R}/R,e_{p_{1}(R)}\otimes f_{p_{2}(R)}^{\ast})

as follows: let
R\leq D\times E
. If there exists a subgroup
Q\leq E
and an
\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{B}^{\ast}
-isomorphism
\psi:\Delta(\phi(Q),\phi,Q)\overset{\sim}{\to}R
set
\theta_{R}:={}^{\psi}\chi_{Q}
; otherwise, set
\theta_{R}=0
. Note that in the former case
\theta_{R}
does not depend on the subgroup
Q
or the isomorphism
\psi
: indeed, suppose that
Q^{\prime}\leq E
and
\psi^{\prime}:\Delta(\phi(Q^{\prime}),\phi,Q^{\prime})\overset{\sim}{\to}R
is another isomorphism in
\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{B}^{\ast}
. Then
\psi^{-1}\psi^{\prime}:\Delta(\phi(Q^{\prime}),\phi,Q^{\prime})\overset{\sim}{\to}\Delta(\phi(Q),\phi,Q)
is an
\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{B}^{\ast}
-isomorphism. Let
(g,h)\in G\times H
be such that
\psi^{-1}\psi^{\prime}=c_{(g,h)}
and

{}^{(g,h)}(\Delta(\phi(Q^{\prime}),\phi,Q^{\prime}),e_{\phi(Q^{\prime})}\otimes f_{Q^{\prime}}^{\ast})=(\Delta(\phi(Q),\phi,Q),e_{\phi(Q)}\otimes f_{Q}^{\ast}).

By definition we have
{}^{\psi^{-1}\psi^{\prime}}\chi_{Q^{\prime}}={}^{(g,h)}\chi_{Q^{\prime}}
. Note that
{}^{g}(\phi(Q^{\prime}),e_{\phi(Q^{\prime})})=(\phi(Q),e_{\phi(Q)})
and
{}^{h}(Q^{\prime},f_{Q^{\prime}})=(Q,f_{Q})
by Lemma
2.7
. Note also that
c_{g}\phi=\phi c_{h}:Q^{\prime}\overset{\sim}{\to}\phi(Q)
. So by condition (1) of Definition
8.2
we have
{}^{(g,h)}\chi_{Q^{\prime}}=\chi_{Q}
, i.e.,
{}^{\psi^{-1}\psi^{\prime}}\chi_{Q^{\prime}}=\chi_{Q}
. Thus
{}^{\psi^{\prime}}\chi_{Q^{\prime}}={}^{\psi}\chi_{Q}
and the definition of
\theta_{R}
does not depend on the choice of
Q
or
\psi
.

We now have a tuple
(\theta_{R})\in\prod_{R\leq D\times E}R_{\mathbb{K}}(X_{R}/R,e_{p_{1}(R)}\otimes f_{p_{2}(R)}^{\ast})
. Note that if
Q\leq E
then
\theta_{\Delta(\phi(Q),\phi,Q)}=\chi_{Q}
. Recall that we have an injective homomorphism

\delta:T(A,B)\hookrightarrow\left(\prod_{R\leq D\times E}R_{\mathbb{K}}(X_{R}/R,e_{p_{1}(R)}\otimes f_{p_{2}(R)}^{\ast})\right)^{\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{B}^{\ast}}

which maps the isomorphism class of a trivial source
A\otimes B^{\ast}
-module
M
to
(\chi_{M(R,e_{p_{1}(R)}\otimes f_{p_{2}(R)}^{\ast})})
, and that by Corollary
7.10
the image of
T^{\Delta}(A,B)
under
\delta
is the subgroup of tuples
(\theta_{R})\in\left(\prod_{R\leq D\times E}R_{\mathbb{K}}(X_{R}/R,e_{p_{1}(R)}\otimes f_{p_{2}(R)}^{\ast})\right)^{\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{B}^{\ast}}
satisfying
\theta_{R}=0
if
R\notin S_{p}^{\Delta}(G\times H)
and the “coherence condition”

\theta_{R}((us,vt)\epsilon_{\langle(u,v)\rangle})=\operatorname{Ind}_{C_{X_{R}\cap X_{R\langle(u,v)\rangle}}(u,v)}^{C_{X_{R}}(u,v)}(\operatorname{Res}_{C_{X_{R}\cap X_{R\langle(u,v)\rangle}}(u,v)}^{X_{R\langle(u,v)\rangle}}(\theta_{R\langle(u,v)\rangle}))(s,t)

(3)

for all
R\leq D\times E
,
(u,v)\in N_{D\times E}(R)
, and
(s,t)\in C_{X_{R}}(u,v)_{p^{\prime}}
, where

\epsilon_{\langle(u,v)\rangle}=\operatorname{tr}_{C_{X_{R}\cap X_{R\langle(u,v)\rangle}}(u,v)}^{C_{X_{R}}(u,v)}(e_{p_{1}(R)\langle u\rangle}\otimes f_{p_{2}(R)\langle v\rangle}^{\ast}).

I claim that the tuple
(\theta_{R})
defined above belongs to
\delta(T^{\Delta}(A,B))
. It is clear that
\theta_{R}=0
if
R
is not a twisted diagonal subgroup of
G\times H
and it is not hard to see that the tuple
(\theta_{R})
is
\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{B}^{\ast}
-fixed: to check this, one needs to show that if
\psi:R\overset{\sim}{\to}S
is an
\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{B}^{\ast}
-isomorphism then
{}^{\psi}\theta_{R}=\theta_{S}
. But if
R
is not
\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{B}^{\ast}
-isomorphic to a subgroup of the form
\Delta(\phi(Q),\phi,Q)
for any
Q\leq E
then neither is
S
, and hence both
\theta_{R}
and
\theta_{S}
are 0, and if there exists a subgroup
Q\leq E
and an
\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{B}^{\ast}
-isomorphism
\psi^{\prime}:\Delta(\phi(Q),\phi,Q)\overset{\sim}{\to}R
then
\psi\psi^{\prime}:\Delta(\phi(Q),\phi,Q)\overset{\sim}{\to}S
is an
\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{B}^{\ast}
-isomorphism and we have

{}^{\psi}\theta_{R}={}^{\psi}({}^{\psi^{\prime}}\chi_{Q})={}^{\psi\psi^{\prime}}\chi_{Q}=\theta_{S}.

So to verify the claim it remains to show that the tuple
(\theta_{R})
satisfies the “coherence condition” (
3
) given above.

Let
R\leq D\times E
,
(u,v)\in N_{D\times E}(R)
, and
(s,t)\in C_{X_{R}}(u,v)_{p^{\prime}}
. Set

\epsilon_{\langle(u,v)\rangle}=\operatorname{tr}_{C_{X_{R}\cap X_{R\langle(u,v)\rangle}}(u,v)}^{C_{X_{R}}(u,v)}(e_{p_{1}(R)\langle u\rangle}\otimes f_{p_{2}(R)\langle v\rangle}^{\ast}).

By Lemma
2.10

(\langle(u,v)\rangle,\epsilon_{\langle(u,v)\rangle})
is an
\mathcal{O}X_{R}(e_{p_{1}(R)}\otimes f_{p_{2}(R)}^{\ast})
-Brauer pair.

Suppose first that
R
is not
\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{B}^{\ast}
-isomorphic to any subgroup of
\Delta(D,\phi,E)
. Then neither is
R\langle(u,v)\rangle
, so
\theta_{R}=0
and
\theta_{R\langle(u,v)\rangle}=0
. In this case, Equation (
3
) clearly holds.

Now suppose that
R\langle(u,v)\rangle
is
\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{B}^{\ast}
-isomorphic to a subgroup of
\Delta(D,\phi,E)
. Let
\psi^{\prime}:\Delta(P^{\prime},\phi,Q^{\prime})\overset{\sim}{\to}R\langle(u,v)\rangle
be an
\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{B}^{\ast}
-isomorphism where
Q^{\prime}\leq E
and
P^{\prime}=\phi(Q^{\prime})
. Write
\Delta(P,\phi,Q)
for the unique subgroup of
\Delta(P^{\prime},\phi,Q^{\prime})
such that
\psi^{\prime}(\Delta(P,\phi,Q))=R
and write
\psi
for the restriction of
\psi^{\prime}
to
\Delta(P,\phi,Q)
. Then
\psi:\Delta(P,\phi,Q)\overset{\sim}{\to}R
is an
\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{B}^{\ast}
-isomorphism. Let us also write
(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})
for the unique element of
\Delta(P^{\prime},\phi,Q^{\prime})
satisfying
\psi^{\prime}((u^{\prime},v^{\prime}))=(u,v)
. Note that
u^{\prime}=\phi(v^{\prime})
, that
(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})\in N_{D\times E}(\Delta(P,\phi,Q))
, and that
\Delta(P^{\prime},\phi,Q^{\prime})=\Delta(P\langle u^{\prime}\rangle,\phi,Q\langle v^{\prime}\rangle)
. Now let
(g,h)\in G\times H
be such that
(\psi^{\prime})^{-1}=c_{(g,h)}
and

\displaystyle{}^{(g,h)}(R\langle(u,v)\rangle,e_{p_{1}(R)\langle u\rangle}\otimes f_{p_{2}(R)\langle v\rangle}^{\ast})

\displaystyle=(\Delta(P^{\prime},\phi,Q^{\prime}),e_{P^{\prime}}\otimes f_{Q^{\prime}}^{\ast})

\displaystyle=(\Delta(P\langle u^{\prime}\rangle,\phi,Q\langle v^{\prime}\rangle),e_{P\langle u^{\prime}\rangle}\otimes f_{Q\langle v^{\prime}\rangle}^{\ast}).

Note that
(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})=({}^{g}u,{}^{h}v)
. Also,
\psi^{-1}=c_{(g,h)}
and

{}^{(g,h)}(R,e_{p_{1}(R)}\otimes f_{p_{2}(R)}^{\ast})=(\Delta(P,\phi,Q),e_{P}\otimes f_{Q}^{\ast}).

It follows that
{}^{(g,h)}(\langle(u,v)\rangle,\epsilon_{\langle(u,v)\rangle})\in\mathcal{BP}_{\mathcal{O}}(Y_{Q},e_{P}\otimes f_{Q}^{\ast})
. In other words, if we set
\epsilon^{\prime}={}^{(g,h)}\epsilon_{\langle(u,v)\rangle}
then
(\langle(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})\rangle,\epsilon^{\prime})
is an
\mathcal{O}Y_{Q}(e_{P}\otimes f_{Q}^{\ast})
-Brauer pair. Note that

\displaystyle\epsilon^{\prime}

\displaystyle={}^{(g,h)}\epsilon_{\langle(u,v)\rangle}

\displaystyle={}^{(g,h)}\operatorname{tr}_{C_{X_{R}\cap X_{R\langle(u,v)\rangle}}(u,v)}^{C_{X_{R}}(u,v)}(e_{p_{1}(R)\langle u\rangle}\otimes f_{p_{2}(R)\langle v\rangle}^{\ast})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{tr}_{C_{Y_{Q}\cap Y_{Q\langle v^{\prime}\rangle}}(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})}^{C_{Y_{Q}}(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})}(e_{P\langle u^{\prime}\rangle}\otimes f_{Q\langle v^{\prime}\rangle}^{\ast}),

so by Lemma
2.10

(\langle(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})\rangle,\epsilon^{\prime})\leq(N_{D\times E}(\Delta(P,\phi,Q)),e_{N_{D}(P)}\otimes f_{N_{E}(Q)}^{\ast})

is a containment of
\mathcal{O}Y_{Q}(e_{P}\otimes f_{Q}^{\ast})
-Brauer pairs. Set
s^{\prime}={}^{g}s
and
t^{\prime}={}^{h}t
. Then
(s^{\prime},t^{\prime})\in C_{Y_{Q}}(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})_{p^{\prime}}
. Condition (2a) of Definition
8.2
then implies that

\chi_{Q}((u^{\prime}s^{\prime},v^{\prime}t^{\prime})\epsilon^{\prime})=\operatorname{Ind}_{C_{Y_{Q}\cap Y_{Q\langle v^{\prime}\rangle}}(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})}^{C_{Y_{Q}}(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})}(\operatorname{Res}_{C_{Y_{Q}\cap Y_{Q\langle v^{\prime}\rangle}}(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})}^{Y_{Q\langle v^{\prime}\rangle}}(\chi_{Q\langle v^{\prime}\rangle}))(s^{\prime},t^{\prime}).

Now
\theta_{R}={}^{\psi}\chi_{Q}={}^{(g,h)^{-1}}\chi_{Q}
and
\theta_{R\langle(u,v)\rangle}={}^{\psi^{\prime}}\chi_{Q^{\prime}}={}^{(g,h)^{-1}}\chi_{Q\langle v^{\prime}\rangle}
, so we can compute:

\displaystyle\theta_{R}((us,vt)\epsilon_{\langle(u,v)\rangle})

\displaystyle={}^{(g,h)^{-1}}\chi_{Q}((us,vt)\epsilon_{\langle(u,v)\rangle})

\displaystyle=\chi_{Q}((u^{\prime}s^{\prime},v^{\prime}t^{\prime})\epsilon^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{Ind}_{C_{Y_{Q}\cap Y_{Q\langle v^{\prime}\rangle}}(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})}^{C_{Y_{Q}}(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})}(\operatorname{Res}_{C_{Y_{Q}\cap Y_{Q\langle v^{\prime}\rangle}}(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})}^{Y_{Q\langle v^{\prime}\rangle}}(\chi_{Q\langle v^{\prime}\rangle}))(s^{\prime},t^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{Ind}_{{}^{(g,h)}C_{X_{R}\cap X_{R\langle(u,v)\rangle}}(u,v)}^{{}^{(g,h)}C_{X_{R}}(u,v)}(\operatorname{Res}_{{}^{(g,h)}C_{X_{R}\cap X_{R\langle(u,v)\rangle}}(u,v)}^{{}^{(g,h)}X_{R\langle(u,v)\rangle}}({}^{(g,h)}\theta_{R\langle(u,v)\rangle}))({}^{g}s,{}^{h}t)

\displaystyle={}^{(g,h)}[\operatorname{Ind}_{C_{X_{R}\cap X_{R\langle(u,v)\rangle}}(u,v)}^{C_{X_{R}}(u,v)}(\operatorname{Res}_{C_{X_{R}\cap X_{R\langle(u,v)\rangle}}(u,v)}^{X_{R\langle(u,v)\rangle}}(\theta_{R\langle(u,v)\rangle}))]({}^{(g,h)}(s,t))

\displaystyle=\operatorname{Ind}_{C_{X_{R}\cap X_{R\langle(u,v)\rangle}}(u,v)}^{C_{X_{R}}(u,v)}(\operatorname{Res}_{C_{X_{R}\cap X_{R\langle(u,v)\rangle}}(u,v)}^{X_{R\langle(u,v)\rangle}}(\theta_{R\langle(u,v)\rangle}))(s,t)

So Equation (
3
) holds in this case as well.

The final case to consider is the case in which
R
is
\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{B}^{\ast}
-isomorphic to a subgroup of
\Delta(D,\phi,E)
but
R\langle(u,v)\rangle
is not. In this case there exists a subgroup
\Delta(P,\phi,Q)\leq\Delta(D,\phi,E)
and an
\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{B}^{\ast}
-isomorphism
\psi:\Delta(P,\phi,Q)\overset{\sim}{\to}R
. We may assume without loss of generality that
Q
is a fully
\mathcal{B}
-normalized subgroup of
E
, for if
Q^{\prime}\in Q^{\mathcal{B}}
is a fully
\mathcal{B}
-normalized subgroup of
E
and
\beta:Q^{\prime}\overset{\sim}{\to}Q
is a
\mathcal{B}
-isomorphism then, setting
P^{\prime}=\phi(Q^{\prime})
, we have that
\alpha:=\phi\beta\phi^{-1}:P^{\prime}\overset{\sim}{\to}P
is an
\mathcal{A}
-isomorphism, hence that
\psi^{\prime}:=(\alpha,\beta):\Delta(P^{\prime},\phi,Q^{\prime})\overset{\sim}{\to}\Delta(P,\phi,Q)
is an
\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{B}^{\ast}
-isomorphism, and so
\psi\psi^{\prime}:\Delta(P^{\prime},\phi,Q^{\prime})\overset{\sim}{\to}R
is an
\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{B}^{\ast}
-isomorphism.

Now since
Q
is fully
\mathcal{B}
-normalized and
\phi:\mathcal{B}\overset{\sim}{\to}\mathcal{A}
is an isomorphism of fusion systems, Lemma
2.9
implies that
\Delta(P,\phi,Q)
is a fully
\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{B}^{\ast}
-normalized subgroup of
D\times E
. In particular, we have that
(N_{D\times E}(\Delta(P,\phi,Q)),e_{N_{D}(P)}\otimes f_{N_{E}(Q)}^{\ast})
is a maximal
\mathcal{O}Y_{Q}(e_{P}\otimes f_{Q}^{\ast})
-Brauer pair by Lemma
2.10
.

Now let
(g,h)\in G\times H
be such that
\psi^{-1}=c_{(g,h)}
and

{}^{(g,h)}(R,e_{p_{1}(R)}\otimes f_{p_{2}(R)}^{\ast})=(\Delta(P,\phi,Q),e_{P}\otimes f_{Q}^{\ast}).

Since
(\langle(u,v)\rangle,\epsilon_{\langle(u,v)\rangle})\in\mathcal{BP}_{\mathcal{O}}(X_{R},e_{p_{1}(R)}\otimes f_{p_{2}(R)}^{\ast})
we have that
{}^{(g,h)}(\langle(u,v)\rangle,\epsilon_{\langle(u,v)\rangle})\in\mathcal{BP}_{\mathcal{O}}(Y_{Q},e_{P}\otimes f_{Q}^{\ast})
. Because
(N_{D\times E}(\Delta(P,\phi,Q)),e_{N_{D}(P)}\otimes f_{N_{E}(Q)}^{\ast})
is a maximal
\mathcal{O}Y_{Q}(e_{P}\otimes f_{Q}^{\ast})
-Brauer pair there exists an element
(x,y)\in Y_{Q}
such that

{}^{(x,y)}({}^{(g,h)}(\langle(u,v)\rangle,\epsilon_{\langle(u,v)\rangle}))\leq(N_{D\times E}(\Delta(P,\phi,Q)),e_{N_{D}(P)}\otimes f_{N_{E}(Q)}^{\ast}).

Set
(g^{\prime},h^{\prime})=(xg,yh)
. Note that
c_{(x,y)}\in\operatorname{Aut}_{\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{B}^{\ast}}(\Delta(P,\phi,Q))
. Set
\psi^{\prime}=\psi\circ c_{(x,y)}^{-1}:\Delta(P,\phi,Q)\overset{\sim}{\to}R
. Note that
\psi^{\prime}
is an
\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{B}^{\ast}
-isomorphism. Note also that
(\psi^{\prime})^{-1}=c_{(g^{\prime},h^{\prime})}
and that

{}^{(g^{\prime},h^{\prime})}(R,e_{p_{1}(R)}\otimes f_{p_{2}(R)}^{\ast})=(\Delta(P,\phi,Q),e_{P}\otimes f_{Q}^{\ast}).

Thus, after replacing
\psi
with
\psi^{\prime}
and
(g,h)
with
(g^{\prime},h^{\prime})
, we may assume without loss of generality that

{}^{(g,h)}(\langle(u,v)\rangle,\epsilon_{\langle(u,v)\rangle})\leq(N_{D\times E}(\Delta(P,\phi,Q)),e_{N_{D}(P)}\otimes f_{N_{E}(Q)}^{\ast}).

Now since
\Delta(P,\phi,Q)
is fully
\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{B}^{\ast}
-normalized also
\Delta(P,\phi,Q)
is receptive in
\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{B}^{\ast}
. Since
{}^{(g,h)}(u,v)\in N_{D\times E}(\Delta(P,\phi,Q))
we have that — in the notation of
[
7
, Part I, Definition 2.2]
—
(u,v)\in N_{\psi^{-1}}
. Therefore
\psi^{-1}
extends to an
\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{B}^{\ast}
-homomorphism defined on
R\langle(u,v)\rangle
. In other words, there exists an element
(g^{\prime},h^{\prime})\in G\times H
such that

{}^{(g^{\prime},h^{\prime})}(R\langle(u,v)\rangle,e_{p_{1}(R)\langle u\rangle}\otimes f_{p_{2}(R)\langle v\rangle}^{\ast})\leq(D\times E,e_{D}\otimes f_{E}^{\ast})

and
\psi^{-1}=c_{(g^{\prime},h^{\prime})}:R\overset{\sim}{\to}\Delta(P,\phi,Q)
. Note that this implies

{}^{(g^{\prime},h^{\prime})}(R,e_{p_{1}(R)}\otimes f_{p_{2}(R)}^{\ast})=(\Delta(P,\phi,Q),e_{P}\otimes f_{Q}^{\ast})

and
{}^{(g^{\prime},h^{\prime})}(u,v)\in N_{D\times E}(\Delta(P,\phi,Q))
. Furthermore

\displaystyle{}^{(g^{\prime},h^{\prime})}\epsilon_{\langle(u,v)\rangle}

\displaystyle={}^{(g^{\prime},h^{\prime})}\operatorname{tr}_{C_{X_{R}\cap X_{R\langle(u,v)\rangle}}(u,v)}^{C_{X_{R}}(u,v)}(e_{p_{1}(R)\langle u\rangle}\otimes f_{p_{2}(R)\langle v\rangle}^{\ast})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{tr}_{C_{Y_{Q}\cap X_{\Delta(P,\phi,Q)\langle({}^{g^{\prime}}u,{}^{h^{\prime}}v)\rangle}}({}^{g^{\prime}}u,{}^{h^{\prime}}v)}^{C_{Y_{Q}}({}^{g^{\prime}}u,{}^{h^{\prime}}v)}(e_{P\langle{}^{g^{\prime}}u\rangle}\otimes f_{Q\langle{}^{h^{\prime}}v\rangle}^{\ast}).

So by Lemma
2.10

{}^{(g^{\prime},h^{\prime})}(\langle(u,v)\rangle,\epsilon_{\langle(u,v)\rangle})\leq(N_{D\times E}(\Delta(P,\phi,Q)),e_{N_{D}(P)}\otimes f_{N_{E}(Q)}^{\ast})

is a containment of
\mathcal{O}Y_{Q}(e_{P}\otimes f_{Q}^{\ast})
-Brauer pairs. Thus we may assume without loss of generality that

{}^{(g,h)}(R\langle(u,v)\rangle,e_{p_{1}(R)\langle u\rangle}\otimes f_{p_{2}(R)\langle v\rangle}^{\ast})\leq(D\times E,e_{D}\otimes f_{E}^{\ast}).

Set
(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})={}^{(g,h)}(u,v)
,
(s^{\prime},t^{\prime})={}^{(g,h)}(s,t)
, and
\epsilon^{\prime}={}^{(g,h)}\epsilon_{\langle(u,v)\rangle}
. Then
((u^{\prime},v^{\prime}),\epsilon^{\prime})\in\mathcal{BE}_{\mathcal{O}}(Y_{Q},e_{P}\otimes f_{Q}^{\ast})
,

(\langle(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})\rangle,\epsilon^{\prime})\leq(N_{D\times E}(\Delta(P,\phi,Q)),e_{N_{D}(P)}\otimes f_{N_{E}(Q)}^{\ast}),

and
(s^{\prime},t^{\prime})\in C_{Y_{Q}}(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})_{p^{\prime}}
. Note that

{}^{(g,h)}(R\langle(u,v)\rangle,e_{p_{1}(R)\langle u\rangle}\otimes f_{p_{2}(R)\langle v\rangle}^{\ast})=(\Delta(P,\phi,Q)\langle(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})\rangle,e_{P\langle u^{\prime}\rangle}\otimes f_{Q\langle v^{\prime}\rangle}^{\ast}).

Now, since we are assuming that
R\langle(u,v)\rangle
is not
\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{B}^{\ast}
-isomorphic to a subgroup of
\Delta(D,\phi,E)
we have
\theta_{R\langle(u,v)\rangle}=0
by definition. So to verify Equation (
3
) in this final case we need to show that
\theta_{R}((us,vt)\epsilon_{\langle(u,v)\rangle})=0
. We have
\theta_{R}={}^{\psi}\chi_{Q}={}^{(g,h)^{-1}}\chi_{Q}
, so
\theta_{R}((us,vt)\epsilon_{\langle(u,v)\rangle})=\chi_{Q}((u^{\prime}s^{\prime},v^{\prime}t^{\prime})\epsilon^{\prime})
. Suppose
\chi_{Q}((u^{\prime}s^{\prime},v^{\prime}t^{\prime})\epsilon^{\prime})\neq 0
. Then by condition (2b) of Definition
8.2
we have

(\Delta(P,\phi,Q)\langle(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})\rangle,e_{P\langle u^{\prime}\rangle}\otimes f_{Q\langle v^{\prime}\rangle}^{\ast})\leq_{G\times H}(\Delta(D,\phi,E),e_{D}\otimes f_{E}^{\ast}).

But this implies that

(R\langle(u,v)\rangle,e_{p_{1}(R)\langle u\rangle}\otimes f_{p_{2}(R)\langle v\rangle}^{\ast})\leq_{G\times H}(\Delta(D,\phi,E),e_{D}\otimes f_{E}^{\ast}),

or in other words, that
R\langle(u,v)\rangle
is
\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{B}^{\ast}
-isomorphic to a subgroup of
\Delta(D,\phi,E)
. So we must have
\chi_{Q}((u^{\prime}s^{\prime},v^{\prime}t^{\prime})\epsilon^{\prime})=0
, hence
\theta_{R}((us,vt)\epsilon_{\langle(u,v)\rangle})=0
and Equation (
3
) holds. This completes the proof of the claim that
(\theta_{R})
belongs to
\delta(T^{\Delta}(A,B))
.

Let
\gamma\in T^{\Delta}(A,B)
be such that
\delta(\gamma)=(\theta_{R})
. Note that for each
Q\leq E
we have

\chi_{Q}=\theta_{\Delta(\phi(Q),\phi,Q)}=\chi_{\gamma(\Delta(\phi(Q),\phi,Q),e_{\phi(Q)}\otimes f_{Q}^{\ast})}

by definition of the map
\delta
. Now Condition (3) of Definition
8.2
implies in particular that
\chi_{Q}\neq 0
for all
Q\leq E
. Therefore
\gamma(\Delta(D,\phi,E),e_{D}\otimes f_{E}^{\ast})\neq 0
, i.e.,
(\Delta(D,\phi,E),e_{D}\otimes f_{E}^{\ast})
is a
\gamma
-Brauer pair. In fact,
(\Delta(D,\phi,E),e_{D}\otimes f_{E}^{\ast})
is a maximal
\gamma
-Brauer pair since any
\gamma
-Brauer pair is an
A\otimes B^{\ast}
-Brauer pair and must have a twisted diagonal subgroup in its first entry.

To complete the proof it remains to show that
\gamma
is a
p
-permutation equivalence. By
[
2
, Theorems 12.2, 12.3]
it suffices to show that any maximal
\gamma
-Brauer pair is
G\times H
-conjugate to
(\Delta(D,\phi,E),e_{D}\otimes f_{E}^{\ast})
. Let
(\Delta(S,\psi,T),e\otimes f^{\ast})\in\mathcal{BP}_{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma)
be maximal. Then

(\Delta(S,\psi,T),e\otimes f^{\ast})\leq_{G\times H}(D\times E,e_{D}\otimes f_{E}^{\ast})

Since
\mathcal{BP}_{\mathcal{O}}(\gamma)
is stable under conjugation we may assume without loss of generality that
(\Delta(S,\psi,T),e\otimes f^{\ast})
is contained in
(D\times E,e_{D}\otimes f_{E}^{\ast})
. Then
S\leq D
,
e=e_{S}
,
T\leq E
, and
f=f_{T}
. By Lemma
8.6
we have

\theta_{\Delta(S,\psi,T)}=\chi_{\gamma(\Delta(S,\psi,T),e_{S}\otimes f_{T}^{\ast})}\neq 0.

It follows that
\Delta(S,\psi,T)
is
\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{B}^{\ast}
-isomorphic to a subgroup of
\Delta(D,\phi,E)
. In other words, there exists an element
(g,h)\in G\times H
such that

{}^{(g,h)}(\Delta(S,\psi,T),e_{S}\otimes f_{T}^{\ast})\leq(\Delta(D,\phi,E),e_{D}\otimes f_{E}^{\ast}).

Since
(\Delta(S,\psi,T),e_{S}\otimes f_{T}^{\ast})
is a maximal
\gamma
-Brauer pair we must have

{}^{(g,h)}(\Delta(S,\psi,T),e_{S}\otimes f_{T}^{\ast})=(\Delta(D,\phi,E),e_{D}\otimes f_{E}^{\ast}).

The proof is complete.
∎

Theorem 8.8
.

Assume Hypotheses
8.1
. Then the construction of Lemma
8.5
defines a bijection from the set of
p
-permutation equivalences
\gamma
such that
(\Delta(D,\phi,E),e_{D}\otimes f_{E}^{\ast})
is a maximal
\gamma
-Brauer pair and the set of strong isotypies between
A
and
B
relative to
(D,e_{D})
,
(E,f_{E})
, and
\phi:E\overset{\sim}{\to}D
.

Proof.

This follows from Lemmas
8.5
and
8.7
.
∎

9
Connection with isotypies

Let
G
and
H
be finite groups,
(\mathbb{K},\mathcal{O},F)
a
p
-modular system large enough for
G\times H
,
A
a block of
\mathcal{O}G
, and let
B
be a block of
\mathcal{O}H
. Assume Hypotheses
8.1
, and the notation set there. In this section we show that a strong isotypy between
A
and
B
restricts to an isotypy between
A
and
B
. The definition of isotypy we will use is the one given in
[
2
, Definition 15.3]
.

Definition 9.1
.

Let
A\in\operatorname{Bl}(\mathcal{O}G)
,
B\in\operatorname{Bl}(\mathcal{O}H)
, and assume Hypotheses
8.1
. An
isotypy
between
A
and
B
is a family of perfect isometries

\mu_{Q}\in R_{\mathbb{K}}(C_{G}(\phi(Q))\times C_{H}(Q),e_{\phi(Q)}\otimes f_{Q}^{\ast}),\qquad Q\leq E

such that the following conditions are satisfied:

(1)

(Equivariance) For every
Q\leq E
and
(g,h)\in G\times H
such that

{}^{(g,h)}(\Delta(\phi(Q),\phi,Q),e_{\phi(Q)}\otimes f_{Q}^{\ast})\leq(\Delta(D,\phi,E),e_{D}\otimes f_{E}^{\ast})

one has
{}^{(g,h)}\mu_{Q}=\mu_{{}^{h}Q}
.

(2)

(Compatibility) For every
Q\leq E
and
v\in C_{E}(Q)
the diagram below commutes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{CF(C_{H}(Q),f_{Q};\mathbb{K})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{CF(C_{G}(P),e_{P};\mathbb{K})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{CF_{p^{\prime}}(C_{H}(Q\langle v\rangle),f_{Q\langle v\rangle};\mathbb{K})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{CF_{p^{\prime}}(C_{G}(P\langle u\rangle),e_{P\langle u\rangle};\mathbb{K})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{Q}\otimes_{C_{H}(Q)}-}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{C_{H}(Q)}^{v,f_{Q\langle v\rangle}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{C_{G}(P)}^{u,e_{P\langle u\rangle}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{Q\langle v\rangle}\otimes_{C_{H}(Q\langle v\rangle)}-}

(4)

where
P=\phi(Q)
and
u=\phi(v)
.

Theorem 9.2
.

Let
A\in\operatorname{Bl}(\mathcal{O}G)
,
B\in\operatorname{Bl}(\mathcal{O}H)
, and assume Hypotheses
8.1
. Let
\chi_{Q}
,
Q\leq E
, be a strong isotypy between
A
and
B
. For each
Q\leq E
set

\mu_{Q}=\operatorname{Res}_{C_{G}(\phi(Q))\times C_{H}(Q)}^{Y_{Q}}(\chi_{Q})\in R_{\mathbb{K}}(C_{G}(\phi(Q))\times C_{H}(Q),e_{\phi(Q)}\otimes f_{Q}^{\ast}).

Then the characters
\mu_{Q}
,
Q\leq E
, form an isotypy between
A
and
B
.

Proof.

Let
Q\leq E
and set
P=\phi(Q)
. Then
\mu_{Q}
is a perfect isometry by Lemma
8.7
and
[
2
, Proposition 11.9]
. If
(g,h)\in G\times H
is such that

{}^{(g,h)}(\Delta(P,\phi,Q),e_{P}\otimes f_{Q}^{\ast})\leq(\Delta(D,\phi,E),e_{D}\otimes f_{E}^{\ast})

then
\phi({}^{h}Q)={}^{g}P
,
{}^{g}(P,e_{P})=({}^{g}P,e_{{}^{g}P})
,
{}^{h}(Q,f_{Q})=({}^{h}Q,f_{{}^{h}Q})
, and
c_{g}\phi=\phi c_{h}:Q\overset{\sim}{\to}{}^{g}P
, so by (1) of Definition
8.2
we have
{}^{(g,h)}\chi_{Q}=\chi_{{}^{h}Q}
. It follows that
{}^{(g,h)}\mu_{Q}=\mu_{{}^{h}Q}
. We have thus shown that the characters
\mu_{Q}
,
Q\leq E
, satisfy the equivariance condition of Definition
9.1
, and all that remains to check is that the compatiblity condition also holds.

Continue to let
Q\leq E
and
P=\phi(Q)
. Let
v\in C_{E}(Q)
and set
u=\phi(v)\in C_{D}(P)
. It suffices to check that the compatiblity condition holds in the case where
Q
is fully
\mathcal{B}
-centralized. Indeed, let
Q^{\prime}
be a fully
\mathcal{B}
-centralized subgroup that is isomorphic in
\mathcal{B}
to
Q
. Then
Q^{\prime}
is receptive, so there exists an element
h\in H
such that
{}^{h}(Q,f_{Q})=(Q^{\prime},f_{Q^{\prime}})
and
{}^{h}(Q\langle v\rangle,f_{Q\langle v\rangle})\leq(E,f_{E})
. Notice that
v^{\prime}:={}^{h}v\in C_{E}(Q^{\prime})
. If we set
P^{\prime}=\phi(Q^{\prime})
then since
\phi:\mathcal{B}\overset{\sim}{\to}\mathcal{A}
is an isomorphism there exists an element
g\in G
such that
{}^{g}(P,e_{P})=(P^{\prime},e_{P^{\prime}})
,
{}^{g}(P\langle u\rangle,e_{P\langle u\rangle})\leq(D,e_{D})
, and
c_{g}=\phi c_{h}\phi^{-1}:P\langle u\rangle\overset{\sim}{\to}P^{\prime}\langle{}^{g}u\rangle
. Note that
u^{\prime}:={}^{g}u=\phi({}^{h}v)\in C_{D}(P^{\prime})
. We have

{}^{(g,h)}\mu_{Q}=\mu_{Q^{\prime}}\qquad\text{and}\qquad{}^{(g,h)}\mu_{Q\langle v\rangle}=\mu_{Q^{\prime}\langle v^{\prime}\rangle}

by equivariance. Now if

d_{C_{G}(P^{\prime})}^{u^{\prime},e_{P^{\prime}\langle u^{\prime}\rangle}}\circ(\mu_{Q^{\prime}}\otimes_{C_{H}(Q^{\prime})}-)=(\mu_{Q^{\prime}\langle v^{\prime}\rangle}\otimes_{C_{H}(Q^{\prime}\langle v^{\prime}\rangle)}-)\circ d_{C_{H}(Q^{\prime})}^{v^{\prime},f_{Q^{\prime}\langle v^{\prime}\rangle}}

then the formulas given in Lemma
3.3
and Corollary
4.4
show that the diagram (
4
) commutes. Thus we are reduced to the case where
Q
is fully
\mathcal{B}
-centralized. In this case
P
is fully
\mathcal{A}
-centralized,
(C_{D}(P),e_{PC_{D}(P)})
is a maximal
\mathcal{O}C_{G}(P)e_{P}
-Brauer pair, and
(C_{E}(Q),f_{QC_{E}(Q)})
is a maximal
\mathcal{O}C_{H}(Q)f_{Q}
-Brauer pair. If
U\leq C_{D}(P)
then
e_{PU}
is the unique block idempotent of
\mathcal{O}C_{C_{G}(P)}(U)=\mathcal{O}C_{G}(PU)
for which
(U,e_{PU})\leq(C_{D}(P),e_{PC_{D}(P)})
is a containment of
\mathcal{O}C_{G}(P)e_{P}
-Brauer pairs. Likewise if
V\leq C_{E}(Q)
then
(V,f_{QV})\leq(C_{E}(Q),f_{QC_{E}(Q)})
is a containment of
\mathcal{O}C_{H}(Q)f_{Q}
-Brauer pairs. By
[
7
, Theorem 3.19(b)]
we have

\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{A}}(P)=\mathcal{F}_{(C_{D}(P),e_{PC_{D}(P)})}(C_{G}(P),e_{P})

and

\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{B}}(Q)=\mathcal{F}_{(C_{E}(Q),f_{QC_{E}(Q)})}(C_{H}(Q),f_{Q}).

In particular, the isomorphism
\phi:\mathcal{B}\overset{\sim}{\to}\mathcal{A}
restricts to an isomorphism
\phi:\mathcal{F}_{(C_{E}(Q),f_{QC_{E}(Q)})}(C_{H}(Q),f_{Q})\to\mathcal{F}_{(C_{D}(P),e_{PC_{D}(P)})}(C_{G}(P),e_{P})
. Let
\mathcal{V}
denote a set of representatives for the
\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{B}}(Q)
-conjugacy classes of elements of
C_{E}(Q)
such that
v\in\mathcal{V}
. Set
\mathcal{U}=\phi(\mathcal{V})
. Then
\mathcal{U}
is a set of representatives for the
\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{A}}(P)
-conjugacy classes of
C_{D}(P)
and
u\in\mathcal{U}
. By Proposition
4.7
the diagram below commutes:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{CF(C_{H}(Q),f_{Q};\mathbb{K})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{CF(C_{G}(P),e_{P};\mathbb{K})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bigoplus_{y\in\mathcal{V}}CF_{p^{\prime}}(C_{H}(Q\langle y\rangle),f_{Q\langle y\rangle};\mathbb{K})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bigoplus_{x\in\mathcal{U}}CF_{p^{\prime}}(C_{G}(P\langle x\rangle),e_{P\langle x\rangle};\mathbb{K})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{Q}\otimes_{C_{H}(Q)}-}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\oplus_{y\in\mathcal{V}}d_{C_{H}(Q)}^{y,f_{Q\langle y\rangle}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\oplus_{x\in\mathcal{U}}d_{C_{G}(P)}^{x,e_{P\langle x\rangle}}}

(5)

where the lower horizontal map is defined by

\sum_{y\in\mathcal{V}}\psi_{y}\mapsto\sum_{x\in\mathcal{U}}\sum_{y\in\mathcal{V}}d_{C_{G}(P)\times C_{H}(Q)}^{(x,y),e_{P\langle x\rangle}\otimes f_{Q\langle y\rangle}^{\ast}}(\mu_{Q})\otimes_{C_{H}(Q\langle y\rangle)}\psi_{y}.

Now let
x\in\mathcal{U}
and let
y\in\mathcal{V}
. Let
\epsilon_{\langle(x,y)\rangle}
denote the
C_{Y_{Q}}(x,y)
-orbit sum of
e_{P\langle x\rangle}\otimes f_{Q\langle y\rangle}^{\ast}
. Then by Lemma
2.10

((x,y),\epsilon_{\langle(x,y)\rangle})\in\mathcal{BE}_{\mathcal{O}}(Y_{Q},e_{P}\otimes f_{Q}^{\ast})
and

(\langle(x,y)\rangle,\epsilon_{\langle(x,y)\rangle})\leq(N_{D\times E}(\Delta(P,\phi,Q)),e_{N_{D}(P)}\otimes f_{N_{E}(Q)}^{\ast}).

Therefore, by condition (2a) of Definition
8.2
, if
x=\phi(y)
then

d_{Y_{Q}}^{(x,y),\epsilon_{\langle(x,y)\rangle}}(\chi_{Q})=d_{C_{Y_{Q}}(x,y)}(\operatorname{Ind}_{C_{Y_{Q}\cap Y_{Q\langle y\rangle}}(x,y)}^{C_{Y_{Q}}(x,y)}(\operatorname{Res}_{C_{Y_{Q}\cap Y_{Q\langle y\rangle}}(x,y)}^{Y_{Q\langle y\rangle}}(\chi_{Q\langle y\rangle}))).

If
x\neq\phi(y)
then by condition (2b) of Definition
8.2
and Lemma
8.3
we have

d_{Y_{Q}}^{(x,y),\epsilon_{\langle(x,y)\rangle}}(\chi_{Q})=0.

Next we observe that

\operatorname{Res}_{C_{G}(P\langle x\rangle)\times C_{H}(Q\langle y\rangle)}^{C_{Y_{Q}}(x,y)}(d_{Y_{Q}}^{(x,y),\epsilon_{\langle(x,y)\rangle}}(\chi_{Q}))

is a class function in
CF_{p^{\prime}}(C_{G}(P\langle x\rangle)\times C_{H}(Q\langle y\rangle),\epsilon_{\langle(x,y)\rangle};\mathbb{K})
whose evaluation at a
p^{\prime}
-element
(s,t)\in[C_{G}(P\langle x\rangle)\times C_{H}(Q\langle y\rangle)]_{p^{\prime}}
is equal to

\displaystyle\chi_{Q}((xs,yt)\epsilon_{\langle(x,y)\rangle})

\displaystyle=\sum_{e\otimes f^{\ast}\in\operatorname{Orb}_{C_{Y_{Q}}(x,y)}(e_{P\langle x\rangle}\otimes f_{Q\langle y\rangle}^{\ast})}\chi_{Q}((xs,yt)(e\otimes f^{\ast}))

\displaystyle=\sum_{e\otimes f^{\ast}\in\operatorname{Orb}_{C_{Y_{Q}}(x,y)}(e_{P\langle x\rangle}\otimes f_{Q\langle y\rangle}^{\ast})}d_{C_{G}(P)\times C_{H}(Q)}^{(x,y),e\otimes f^{\ast}}(\operatorname{Res}_{C_{G}(P)\times C_{H}(Q)}^{Y_{Q}}(\chi_{Q}))(s,t)

\displaystyle=\sum_{e\otimes f^{\ast}\in\operatorname{Orb}_{C_{Y_{Q}}(x,y)}(e_{P\langle x\rangle}\otimes f_{Q\langle y\rangle}^{\ast})}d_{C_{G}(P)\times C_{H}(Q)}^{(x,y),e\otimes f^{\ast}}(\mu_{Q})(s,t).

It follows that

(e_{P\langle x\rangle}\otimes f_{Q\langle y\rangle}^{\ast})\operatorname{Res}_{C_{G}(P\langle x\rangle)\times C_{H}(Q\langle y\rangle)}^{C_{Y_{Q}}(x,y)}(d_{Y_{Q}}^{(x,y),\epsilon_{\langle(x,y)\rangle}}(\chi_{Q}))=d_{C_{G}(P)\times C_{H}(Q)}^{(x,y),e_{P\langle x\rangle}\otimes f_{Q\langle y\rangle}^{\ast}}(\mu_{Q}).

In particular, if
x\neq\phi(y)
then

d_{C_{G}(P)\times C_{H}(Q)}^{(x,y),e_{P\langle x\rangle}\otimes f_{Q\langle y\rangle}^{\ast}}(\mu_{Q})=0

and if
x=\phi(y)
then one computes via the Mackey formula that

d_{C_{G}(P)\times C_{H}(Q)}^{(x,y),e_{P\langle x\rangle}\otimes f_{Q\langle y\rangle}^{\ast}}(\mu_{Q})=d_{C_{G}(P\langle x\rangle)\times C_{H}(Q\langle y\rangle)}(\mu_{Q\langle y\rangle})

It follows that the lower horizontal map in the diagram (
5
) maps a class function
\psi_{y}\in CF_{p^{\prime}}(C_{H}(Q\langle y\rangle),f_{Q\langle y\rangle};\mathbb{K})
to

\displaystyle\psi_{y}

\displaystyle\mapsto\sum_{x\in\mathcal{U}}d_{C_{G}(P)\times C_{H}(Q)}^{(x,y),e_{P\langle x\rangle}\otimes f_{Q\langle y\rangle}^{\ast}}(\mu_{Q})\otimes_{C_{H}(Q\langle y\rangle)}\psi_{y}

\displaystyle=d_{C_{G}(P\langle\phi(y)\rangle)\times C_{H}(Q\langle y\rangle)}(\mu_{Q\langle y\rangle})\otimes\psi_{y}

\displaystyle=\mu_{Q\langle y\rangle}\otimes_{C_{H}(Q\langle y\rangle)}\psi_{y}.

Letting
x=u
and
y=v
, the above implies that the diagram (
4
) of the compatibility condition commutes. The proof is complete.
∎

10
Commutative diagrams from a strong isotypy

Continue to let
G
and
H
be finite groups,
(\mathbb{K},\mathcal{O},F)
a
p
-modular system large enough for
G\times H
,
A
a block of
\mathcal{O}G
, and let
B
be a block of
\mathcal{O}H
. Assume Hypotheses
8.1
and the notation set there. In this section we describe a commutative diagram that is induced from a strong isotypy and which extends the commutative diagram of the compatibility condition in the definition of “isotypy.”

Lemma 10.1
.

Let
A\in\operatorname{Bl}(\mathcal{O}G)
and
B\in\operatorname{Bl}(\mathcal{O}H)
. Assume Hypotheses
8.1
and let
\chi_{Q}
,
Q\leq E
, be a strong isotypy between
A
and
B
. Let
v\in E
and set
u=\phi(v)
. Then the diagram below commutes:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{CF(H,B;\mathbb{K})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{CF(G,A;\mathbb{K})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{CF_{p^{\prime}}(C_{H}(v),f_{\langle v\rangle};\mathbb{K})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{CF_{p^{\prime}}(C_{G}(u),e_{\langle u\rangle};\mathbb{K})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\chi_{\left\{1\right\}}\otimes_{H}-}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{H}^{v,f_{\langle v\rangle}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{G}^{u,e_{\langle u\rangle}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Res}_{C_{G}(u)\times C_{H}(v)}^{Y_{\langle v\rangle}}(\chi_{\langle v\rangle})\otimes_{C_{H}(v)}-}

(6)

Moreover,
\chi_{\left\{1\right\}}
and
\operatorname{Res}_{C_{G}(u)\times C_{H}(v)}^{Y_{\langle v\rangle}}(\chi_{\langle v\rangle})
are perfect isometries.

Proof.

This is a consequence of Theorem
9.2
and an application of the compatibility condition in Definition
9.1
with
Q=\left\{1\right\}
.
∎

Theorem 10.2
.

Let
A\in\operatorname{Bl}(\mathcal{O}G)
and
B\in\operatorname{Bl}(\mathcal{O}H)
. Assume Hypotheses
8.1
and let
\chi_{Q}
,
Q\leq E
, be a strong isotypy between
A
and
B
. Fix a subgroup
Q\leq E
and set
P=\phi(Q)
. For each subgroup
U\leq N_{D}(P)
write
\epsilon_{U}
for the unique block idempotent of
\mathcal{O}C_{I_{P}}(U)
such that
(U,\epsilon_{U})\leq(N_{D}(P),e_{N_{D}(P)})
is a containment of
\mathcal{O}I_{P}e_{P}
-Brauer pairs and for each
V\leq N_{E}(Q)
write
\varphi_{V}
for the unique block idempotent of
\mathcal{O}C_{J_{Q}}(V)
such that
(V,\varphi_{V})\leq(N_{E}(Q),f_{N_{E}(Q)})
is a containment of
\mathcal{O}J_{Q}f_{Q}
-Brauer pairs (see Lemma
2.10
). Let
v\in N_{E}(Q)
and set
u=\phi(v)\in N_{D}(P)
. Let
\chi_{Q\langle v\rangle}^{\prime}
denote the character

\chi_{Q\langle v\rangle}^{\prime}=\operatorname{Ind}_{C_{Y_{Q}\cap Y_{Q\langle v\rangle}}(u,v)}^{C_{I_{P}}(u)\times C_{J_{Q}}(v)}\operatorname{Res}_{C_{Y_{Q}\cap Y_{Q\langle v\rangle}}(u,v)}^{Y_{Q\langle v\rangle}}\chi_{Q\langle v\rangle}.

Then
\operatorname{Ind}_{Y_{Q}}^{I_{P}\times J_{Q}}(\chi_{Q})
and
\chi_{Q\langle v\rangle}^{\prime}
are perfect isometries and the diagram below commutes:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{CF(J_{Q},f_{Q};\mathbb{K})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{CF(I_{P},e_{P};\mathbb{K})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{CF_{p^{\prime}}(C_{J_{Q}}(v),\varphi_{\langle v\rangle};\mathbb{K})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{CF_{p^{\prime}}(C_{I_{P}}(u),\epsilon_{\langle u\rangle};\mathbb{K})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Ind}_{Y_{Q}}^{I_{P}\times J_{Q}}(\chi_{Q})\otimes_{J_{Q}}-}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{J_{Q}}^{v,\varphi_{\langle v\rangle}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{I_{P}}^{u,\epsilon_{\langle u\rangle}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\chi_{Q\langle v\rangle}^{\prime}\otimes_{C_{J_{Q}}(v)}-}

(7)

Moreover, if
v\in C_{E}(Q)
then the diagrams (
4
) and (
7
) form a commutative cube:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces CF(C_{H}(Q),f_{Q};\mathbb{K})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces CF(C_{G}(P),e_{P};\mathbb{K})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces CF(J_{Q},f_{Q};\mathbb{K})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces CF(I_{P},e_{P};\mathbb{K})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces CF_{p^{\prime}}(C_{H}(Q\langle v\rangle,f_{Q\langle v\rangle};\mathbb{K})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces CF_{p^{\prime}}(C_{G}(P\langle u\rangle),e_{P\langle u\rangle};\mathbb{K})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces CF_{p^{\prime}}(C_{J_{Q}}(v),\varphi_{\langle v\rangle};\mathbb{K})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces CF_{p^{\prime}}(C_{I_{P}}(u),\epsilon_{\langle u\rangle};\mathbb{K})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Res}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Res}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{Q\langle v\rangle}\operatorname{Res}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e_{P\langle u\rangle}\operatorname{Res}}

Proof.

We may assume without loss of generality that
Q
is fully
\mathcal{B}
-normalized. Then
P
is fully
\mathcal{A}
-normalized. By Lemma
2.10

(N_{D}(P),e_{N_{D}(P)})
is a maximal
\mathcal{O}I_{P}e_{P}
-Brauer pair and
(N_{E}(Q),f_{N_{E}(Q)})
is a maximal
\mathcal{O}J_{Q}f_{Q}
-Brauer pair. Furthermore,

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{A}}(P)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{F}_{(N_{D}(P),e_{N_{D}(P)})}(I_{P},e_{P})

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{B}}(Q)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{F}_{(N_{E}(Q),f_{N_{E}(Q)})}(J_{Q},f_{Q}),

and
\phi:\mathcal{B}\overset{\sim}{\to}\mathcal{A}
restricts to an isomorphism
\phi:\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{B}}(Q)\overset{\sim}{\to}\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{A}}(P)
.

Let
\gamma\in T^{\Delta}(A,B)
be the unique
p
-permutation equivalence with maximal
\gamma
-Brauer pair
(\Delta(D,\phi,E),e_{D}\otimes f_{E}^{\ast})
mapping to
(\chi_{Q})_{Q\leq E}
under the bijection in Theorem
8.8
. Set

\gamma_{Q}=\operatorname{Ind}_{Y_{Q}}^{I_{P}\times J_{Q}}(\gamma(\Delta(P,\phi,Q),e_{P}\otimes f_{Q}^{\ast}))\in T^{\Delta}(\mathcal{O}I_{P}e_{P},\mathcal{O}J_{Q}f_{Q}).

Then
\gamma_{Q}
is a
p
-permutation equivalence between
\mathcal{O}I_{P}e_{P}
and
\mathcal{O}J_{Q}f_{Q}
by
[
2
, Theorem 11.4]
, and
(\Delta(N_{D}(P),\phi,N_{E}(Q)),e_{N_{D}(P)}\otimes f_{N_{E}(Q)}^{\ast})
is a maximal
\gamma_{Q}
-Brauer pair by
[
2
, Proposition 11.5(b)]
. For each
V\leq N_{E}(Q)
set

Z_{V}=N_{I_{P}\times J_{Q}}(\Delta(\phi(V),\phi,V),\epsilon_{\phi(V)}\otimes\varphi_{V}^{\ast})

and set

\theta_{V}=\chi_{\gamma_{Q}(\Delta(\phi(V),\phi,V),\epsilon_{\phi(V)}\otimes\varphi_{V}^{\ast})}.

Then by Lemma
8.5
the characters
\theta_{V}
,
V\leq N_{E}(Q)
form a strong isotypy between
\mathcal{O}I_{P}e_{P}
and
\mathcal{O}J_{Q}f_{Q}
. Therefore by Lemma
10.1
the diagram below commutes:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{CF(J_{Q},f_{Q};\mathbb{K})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{CF(I_{P},e_{P};\mathbb{K})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{CF_{p^{\prime}}(C_{J_{Q}}(v),\varphi_{\langle v\rangle};\mathbb{K})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{CF_{p^{\prime}}(C_{I_{P}}(u),\epsilon_{\langle u\rangle};\mathbb{K})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta_{\left\{1\right\}}\otimes_{J_{Q}}-}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Res}_{C_{I_{P}}(u)\times C_{J_{Q}}(v)}^{Z_{\langle v\rangle}}(\theta_{\langle v\rangle})\otimes_{C_{J_{Q}}(v)}-}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{J_{Q}}^{v,\varphi_{\langle v\rangle}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{I_{P}}^{u,\epsilon_{\langle u\rangle}}}

The commutativity of the diagram (
7
) follows after noting that
\theta_{\left\{1\right\}}=\operatorname{Ind}_{Y_{Q}}^{I_{P}\times J_{Q}}(\chi_{Q})
and
\operatorname{Res}_{C_{I_{P}}(u)\times C_{J_{Q}}(v)}^{Z_{\langle v\rangle}}(\theta_{\langle v\rangle})=\chi_{Q\langle v\rangle}^{\prime}
. The final statement regarding the “commutative cube” is easy to verify.
∎
